mr president, ‘let us not allow fear to guide us’, we often say. some people question whether our airport security measures are too tight. well, the detroit terror attempt made us realise that our security systems are not excessively tight, but are still rather unsatisfactory. as you all know, the slovakian secret service placed plastic explosives in the bag of a slovakian citizen. the passenger, as well as the bag, got on board without any problems and landed in ireland. no doubt the test was spectacular; but certainly it did not strengthen the belief of citizens that security on the one hand and the right to privacy on the other hand are taken seriously by the authorities. citizens are flooded with conflicting information and misinterpretations in the press on a daily basis. after detroit, to give you an example, the spotlight is on body scanners as the best solution. mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, in principle, the union is founded on the principles of liberty, democracy, respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms and the rule of law, which are common to the member states. but unfortunately only in principle, because slovakia violates the fundamental principles of the european union on a daily basis, and that is why i am calling on the european parliament to take the necessary legal steps to suspend slovakia's membership of the eu until it withdraws its extremely racist language law that humiliates hungarians on a daily basis. but in the carpathian basin, unfortunately, not only slovakia is in violation of the principles of the european union, but also in romania there is a political offensive to suppress the hungarian minorities. the exclusion of the hungarians in the region of over 300,000 people has made it time to make the issue of self-determination in the region of mýrdal, in addition to the autonomy of the szeklerland, the subject of public discussion.
mr president, the spanish presidency is in fact launching a new institution in the european union – the trio presidency. there have of course been earlier trios, but this is the first to have elaborated a coordinated programme and, equally, the first under the lisbon treaty. the two other member states making up the trio presidency are belgium and hungary. from the hungarian perspective, what is particularly valuable in the new institution is the opportunity to offer our own distinctive contribution to the process. among other questions to be dealt with by the trio presidency and of particular concern is the growing problem of water shortage in europe. for the first time in its history, europe is facing a potential water shortage. given its strategic location, hungary is uniquely placed to put this issue on the european agenda.
three short questions. first, the scientist who led iran's nuclear research was recently murdered in a brutal act of terrorism. what is the european union's position on this issue? interestingly, that was not the case today: i missed her very much. second, why exactly is iran's nuclear capability a bigger problem, a bigger threat to peace, than, say, israel's? why is the european union not doing something about it? third: in hungary, in 2006, the then socialist and liberal government fired into a crowd of peaceful protesters. including the eye safety of 14 people. many people have gone blind. despite our repeated requests, the european union was not willing to deal with this at the time, nor has it since. what's the difference? nor do they deal with the hundreds of political prisoners in hungarian prisons. there are still dozens of political prisoners in hungary. thank you very much. i'll wait for your answer.
it was a shock to me when i was at a food conference and i saw a sign in the hands of the african delegation that said, "don't feed africa". we better realize that africa doesn't need aid, it needs food self-sufficiency. in a peculiar way, the interest of the african farmer and the african population is the same as the interest of the farmers and the population of, say, the post-communist countries of eastern europe, or it is absurd for the french farmer and the population to have the local farmers themselves decide what they want to produce, how they want to produce it, and sell it on the local market. and it's in the interest of the local population to have access to locally produced, healthy, high-quality food. two different logics, the logic of liberalized global free trade and the logic of food self-sufficiency. we should be much more firm in our support for food self-determination.
as a hungarian and a criminal lawyer, i should be ashamed that in two cases, when speaking here in the european parliament today about the lack of legal guarantees in criminal proceedings, my colleague, who is also of a different political persuasion, mentioned hungary as a deterrent example. although i am ashamed, i am forced to agree with them because i have had similar experiences myself and i ask them and all of you, the few representatives who are still here in the room when this important issue is being discussed, to think through that if such serious violations were experienced by foreigners who are completely indifferent to the hungarian state and the hungarian government politically, then what must be the position of those who are opponents of the hungarian government because they are political opponents of the government. now 15 people are in prison for nine months in pretrial detention, who tried to speak out against the corruption cases of the hungarian government. they were retaliated against, incidentally, by a terrorism charge, a criminal charge that has no basis in fact. to this day, no evidence has been found, because the authorities feel no obligation to flag any evidence, and they are being held in pre-trial detention, under circumstances such as those you have experienced, away from their families, the public, the press. i ask that we all come together and do something to normalize the situation in hungary, and that the lack of legal guarantees for criminal proceedings cannot be abused for political reasons, and that such guarantees should be in place in hungary.
in writing. although two existing european laws deal with trafficking in human beings and its victims, in practice eu member states often regard these persons as illegal immigrants. it's very important to distinguish between the two. illegal immigrants are often forced by economic or social circumstances to leave their own country and arrive illegally in the eu by their own choice. but those involved in trafficking do not have a free, informed decision about this. they are to be treated as victims. eu member states need to provide adequate protection for victims. not only should they be provided with legal or physical protection, but also medical, psychological support, social rehabilitation and residence permits for those who cooperate with the authorities while the trafficking case is being investigated. it is also important that the commission, through information campaigns, helps people who are potentially at risk to know their rights, opportunities and risks, both within the eu and in third countries, and that it does everything possible to ensure that member states properly transpose and implement the relevant european law. given that the issue of trafficking in human beings is also closely linked to the remit of several commissioners, including the commissioner for home affairs and justice, the commissioner for human rights and the commissioner for labour, it would be worthwhile to consider the appointment of an interdepartmental coordinator to effectively mediate in order to address the problem appropriately.
in writing. – women and children are the principal victims of human trafficking. when a new council framework decision will be formulated regarding human trafficking, women and children have to be put at the centre of action. therefore i agree with opinions stating that gender-specific violence data must be collected throughout the european union as soon as possible. victim protection costs money, and this life-saving money should be spent wisely. we should bear in mind that without reliable and comparable data we will not be able to locate resources to the appropriate places in an appropriate manner. we also have to be aware of the fact that different member states and especially different cultures handle the problem differently. there are member states where victim protection is well organised and reachable for everyone, like in spain, and there are member states where it is almost a non-issue. that means that we not only need to locate resources wisely, but we also have to come up with practical and statistical solutions (namely with a minimum european standard at least) in order to tackle the latency problem, and in order to draw attention to this question where it is needed.
it is very significant, and symbolic, that the prime minister of a country which now has the highest unemployment rate in the european union is talking about the importance of job creation. i would therefore like to ask the president of the spanish government what are the most important measures, very specifically, to achieve the objective of reducing unemployment, not only in spain but also in europe. the spanish government's programme is enormously ambitious. it wants to adopt a new strategy for growth and employment. at the same time, the lisbon programme, which envisaged the transformation of europe into the most competitive region in the world, is being forgotten. how can a new strategy be envisaged and prepared within a month if the reasons for the failure of the previous programme are not analysed beforehand?
i would like to highlight the common objectives of recovering from the economic crisis and creating jobs. we all know that there are already signs of recovery in some countries, but this is not yet felt in the employment situation. in the union, citizens need jobs most of all. this is the only way to renew the lisbon strategy. we must ensure that the new eu 2020 programme does not repeat the mistakes of the lisbon strategy. we don't know yet what makes it different, more authentic than its predecessor. so far, all we're hearing is some pretty big slogans and a very tight approval process. if we involve the european parliament in the process, it is impossible to complete the procedure before the end of the semester. what is needed is a mature and coordinated program, not a sudden, confused communication effort. so let us consider the following. how do we divide responsibility between the member states and the union? how do we monitor the implementation of the strategy? who are we trying to position with this new strategy? how will it represent the interests of all the regions of the union, rather than just certain sectors, companies or countries? how do we combine cohesion and structural policy? i'm sure there's no time to answer all these questions in a hurry. haste is the price of quality. i would like to draw attention to two issues of pan-european importance among the objectives of the forthcoming hungarian presidency. one is that we are preparing a sustainable european water management strategy, because water is a common resource. this is linked to the danube as a green corridor concept. the other objective is to position the european regions. we need to tap into the power of these communities.
i too would like to wish the spanish presidency, the first of the trio of presidencies under the treaty of lisbon, every success. the 2020 strategy has been repeatedly described as key to overcoming the crisis. however, this is a matter for the member states, whose policies are within their competence, and they bear a very heavy responsibility. i would like to point out that we have community policies, we have a community budget, which can also serve as a means of reducing the effects of the crisis. in this respect, it is very important that the negotiations on the new budgetary period start as early as 2011 and that the commission submits its proposal in this respect so that we can develop these policies, and i would ask the commission to make sure that this happens in time.

in writing. i was pleased to note that the objectives of the spanish presidency include the promotion of equal opportunities for men and women, both in the european labour market and in agricultural development and in relations with third countries. it is also welcome that the programme focuses on combating gender-based violence and protecting victims of such crimes, and that it highlights, among the general objectives, the role of gender equality in the growth of the european union. it is a very important development that in the chapter on action against discrimination, the presidency promises to reduce the gender pay gap, to promote the roadmap for gender equality 2011-2015, to set up a european observatory on gender-based violence and to organise a forum to assess the achievements and future challenges of the un's beijing plan of action. while the programme does not mention its plans for the social inclusion of roma, i sincerely hope that the presidency will continue the work started by its predecessors and, in particular, in the european year for combating poverty and social exclusion, will make every effort to promote the social and economic integration of europe's largest and most vulnerable minority, the roma. i also hope that the spanish presidency will make a significant contribution to the summit in córdoba on international roma day, so that we can bring the emerging european roma strategy to a head as soon as possible.

madam minister! i have some surprising news for you. the copenhagen climate summit is over. you spoke of goals, of illusions, as if we were still ahead of it. the result is failure. no eligible signed commitments were made. and a good example of what volunteering means is kyoto. we know what the commitment of the united states means, but canada's example is worth mentioning. he signed the kyoto protocol, then he increased carbon emissions by 26 percent without any consequences. copenhagen is an unmistakable message about the world we live in. the world today is defined by economic fundamentalism. when human social interest conflicts with the interest of the global economy, the latter always prevails. of course, behind the global economy is a narrow power elite. to prevent irreversible climate change, another climate change is needed. to change the political and moral climate. until that happens, until people are for the economy and not the other way around, until the eco-social market economy becomes the accepted guiding principle, then other conferences like this are doomed to fail.

mr president, i would like to thank you for your service. let's not sugarcoat it. copenhagen is a failure. but i don't want to deal with that. i want to deal with the fact that instead of criticizing others, we sweep our own front yard. let's face it, one of the reasons for the failure was that there was no common european union position in copenhagen. not on the co2 quotas, not on the financing. i agree with those who say that a prerequisite for successful negotiations is a united european union position. that's right, you did. what do you need? this requires that both the commission and all the member states of the union adhere to the letter and spirit of kyoto. hungary, poland, romania, bulgaria, the baltic states, slovakia and the czech republic have exceeded their kyoto commitments. that's why they can sell their co2 allowances. it is not fair, it is not fair, it is not right for the commission and the other member states of the european union to want to deprive these countries of this opportunity. in copenhagen, poland and hungary made a compromise proposal. the idea is to keep the quota sale option after 2012, but to sell a certain amount each year and use the money for green investments. we have made a compromise proposal for a common european union position. now it's the commission and the 15. moreover, if only because without hungary, poland, romania and the other countries, the 15 would not be satisfied with the fact that we have fulfilled our commitment, since instead of the 8% commitment, they would only be at 5.5%. thank you for your attention.
in writing. the current liberal trade rules encourage the maximum carbon emissions. developed countries are making significant efforts to limit their carbon emissions by introducing emissions trading, promoting renewable energy sources and investing in energy efficiency. these measures represent additional costs for the companies. energy consumption in these countries is also being hit by increasing taxes. meanwhile, many developing countries are doing nothing to reduce their greenhouse gas emissions. in fact, it's continually increasing its carbon dioxide emissions. instead of encouraging energy efficiency, it provides electricity at a discount to the largest industrial users. many people now say that the main comparative advantage of these countries is not cheap labor, but cheap energy. new international trade rules should therefore be introduced to prevent trade from leading to further carbon emissions. we need not just fair trade, but green trade. to that end, effective legal sanctions should be allowed to be applied if a country fails to comply with its commitments under climate agreements.
in writing. the copenhagen climate summit has not lived up to expectations. no binding commitments were made to define the future framework for international climate action. the situation is aggravated by the fact that the european union, contrary to its intentions, was unable to exert a significant influence on the outcome of the negotiations. the failure of the summit cannot be a reason for inaction. we have to accept the idea that until we have a binding international treaty for all, the focus will be on regional solutions. accordingly, the european union should also work to further fine-tune its own internal climate protection regulation. the eu legislation needs to be made more effective so as not to put european industries at a competitive disadvantage. the eu's current climate policy should not, however, put the new member states at a disadvantage, as was the case with the recently adopted internal burden-sharing legislation. in climate action, we must look for solutions that have many other side effects in addition to reducing emissions. urban transport improves quality of life, energy efficiency investments create jobs and r & d funds improve competitiveness. these aspects should be reflected in the next seven-year budget. europe cannot afford to be the victim of international climate negotiations. it must continue to play a leading role. even if it's the era of small steps in climate policy instead of big fights.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the danube is one of the key ecological corridors of hungary and the union, the seventh corridor of the pan-european corridors. the role of the danube river is therefore of paramount importance, and jobbik is therefore supporting the international commitments previously undertaken by the republic of hungary in order to implement a high priority project designated by the eu commission until 2020. this development is well in line with the concept of the motorway of the sea, which was also envisaged by the union in the transport white paper. but i would like to draw my colleagues' attention to the fact that, unlike others, the movement for jobbik hungary does not want to do this for profit, but with the utmost respect for international environmental rules and regulations, so as to ensure the optimal width of the channel for the water to drain and the amount of water required for river navigation. without thereby damaging the water bases and natural values along the danube. we are convinced that the danube region can remain a stable region of europe only if we abolish the beneš decrees that discriminate against germans, austrians and hungarians once and for all. thank you for your attention.
madam president, fellow members of the house of representatives, it's a popular wisdom often quoted by politicians that we don't inherit the earth from our grandparents, we borrow it from our grandchildren. it is our responsibility to ensure that our children, our grandchildren, our great-grandchildren can breathe clean air, drink clean water. beneath the danube's banks lies a vast treasure of fresh water. my priority, and hungary's priority, is to protect this freshwater from all kinds of pollution. transportation, tourism, culture, preservation of tradition, all of these are important, but for me, the preservation of the water treasure comes before all of these. i don't know if the initiative for the danube region, which we are discussing right now, will be successful. what we agree on now are more principles, more evidence. but as we know, the devil is in the details. so i want to make it clear right now that if we're going to go beyond the principle and start arguing about the details, for me, there's a principle that i'm not going to let go of. the danube can only be used for interventions, investments, developments, that's my position, that don't threaten drinking water sources. i would like to look my children and grandchildren in the eye and tell them, without blushing, that the danube, and this is true of other european rivers, such as the tisa, has been preserved as we borrowed it from them. we didn't destroy it, so they can use it.

madam president, i would like to thank you for your kind words. freshwater and food will be as strategic commodities in a decade or two as oil and gas are today. water management and water conservation are therefore of great importance for the european union as a whole, but especially for the danube region. we are currently struggling with floods, with floods, and at the same time, these waters are flowing over us, and you can see, for example, between the danube and the tisza in hungary, in the sand dunes, a very serious desertification. the danube strategy is therefore an important priority for the hungarian eu presidency in 2011. that is why i am pleased that today's debate has shown that, in a complex approach, we can simultaneously promote regional policy, environmental protection, navigability, economic development, job creation and tourism, and that the danube - as today's debate shows - can bring about reconciliation and peace between states that are in a state of mutually distorting tension, as the poet joseph attila said.

ladies and gentlemen, 4% gdp decline, 21 million eu citizens unemployed, 20 member states are facing deficit procedures, the national debt is 80%. in view of the situation in the eu, i ask: can we afford the luxury of billions of taxpayers going unchecked? it is unacceptable that, while we are making a lot of efforts to preserve jobs and stimulate the economy, there has been no progress at eu level so far, for example on how to promote the recovery of cross-border tax debts from the shameful 5% level. or whether we can extend automatic exchange of information uniformly across all income streams, which would avoid governments having to look for stolen media about the untaxed income of their citizens being placed here and there. the scale of tax fraud in the union is currently two and a half times the size of the total eu budget. i believe that the tax authorities of the member states should cooperate in order to detect tax fraud. we must now stop hiding behind bank notes and eliminate the quasi-tax havens within the union. even if it hurts the member states concerned. the european interest must take precedence over partial considerations. eu citizens who pay fairly expect us to make the rules binding on everyone, not loopholes. the domenici report considers how these loopholes could be closed. it is not tax harmonization that is on the agenda, but the ability of each member state to collect its own taxes, if necessary with the help of the others. all the other elements of the tax package before us are designed to do just that. i have attached to the domenici report, on behalf of the european people's party, a number of proposals which have been supported by the other groups. first, i initiated the creation of an incentive scheme to ensure that, when cross-border tax debts are recovered, the member state acting in favour of the other country also shares the tax collected. this could give a boost to the currently fledgling cooperation between tax authorities. secondly, the comparative profit method could be particularly effective against multinationals that operate with transfer pricing for the purpose of tax avoidance. i know that commissioner kovács has some doubts about this, but i think we can start work. finally, i am pleased that the commission supports the idea that the tax information requirement for 12 countries under the oecd model convention can be tightened to an agreement requirement. i think these are the paths we can take to a more equitable tax policy.

in writing. the main task of the european union's policy on promoting equality between men and women is to assess the successes and failures of the four-year roadmap that expires this year and to define the outlines of the new strategy. the roadmap for the future should as in the present place adequate emphasis on the phenomenon of multiple and sectorial discrimination and pay greater attention to the women's rights aspects of the situation of ethnic minorities. the report rightly points to the adverse effects of the global economic crisis on the situation of women, in particular in terms of employment and the gender pay gap. it is therefore essential that the new strategy takes account of the economic importance of gender equality, as gender exclusion is not only unfair but also economically disadvantageous. member states and the undertakings concerned should therefore be encouraged to integrate the gender dimension in their crisis management strategies and to avoid financial restrictions that are detrimental to equality of opportunity between men and women. the post-2010 roadmap should maintain the priorities of the previous strategy and give greater attention to poverty and social exclusion, especially in the context of the european year dedicated to this objective. the new strategy must be a real action plan with realistic, measurable objectives and much more effective coordination between the european commission and the member states in implementing them.

madam president, i should like to welcome commissioner füle and the minister. everybody will be pleased by the progress that croatia is making in the completion of its accession process. key areas of governance must be adapted to the requirements of the acquis, and some of these changes – let us be clear about this – are likely to go against the grain of tradition and expectations. so, negotiating the transformation demands a major act of political will. let me add that the effort is well worthwhile, especially for a relatively small state like croatia or, for that matter – and the same applies – for the other states of the western balkans. eu membership – i think we can take this for granted – provides a set of advantages in political, economic, cultural and security terms. the greatest problem of adaptation, though, lies elsewhere. it is one thing to change the structures of governance but it is a very different matter to change the attitudes of society to something radically different to the forms and content that have been developed in the european union. the two are frequently far from each other, and there will certainly be elements in society, some quite powerful ones at that, which will see only disadvantages for themselves in the new dispensation. let there be no illusions about this. the croatian authorities have not only to complete their negotiations with the european union but, at the same time, they must also do what they can to change social attitudes. that may turn out to be the harder task.

in writing. − trafficking in human beings is one of the most serious forms of human rights violations, taking many forms, ranging from sexual exploitation and forced labour to organ trafficking and domestic servitude, and particularly affecting women and children. the community legal framework for combating trafficking in human beings is currently inadequate and it is therefore essential that the european union, on the basis of the powers conferred on it by the treaty of lisbon, take much stronger action against the phenomenon, in particular with a view to protecting and assisting groups already victims or at risk, in particular children. in this respect, the initiative to establish a european coordination post against trafficking in human beings and the call on member states to impose penalties that are dissuasive and reflect the seriousness of the crime are welcome. as a very important development, the motion for a resolution states that the consent of the victim to exploitation is irrelevant to the prosecution and that the victim is entitled to assistance regardless of his willingness to participate in the proceedings. it is also important to involve civil society as fully as possible in institutional activities to combat trafficking in human beings and to initiate information and awareness-raising campaigns for the most vulnerable groups. it is hoped that member states will soon transpose an integrated approach to prevention, retaliation and protection of victims into their domestic legal systems and take a major step towards the eradication of modern slavery by ratifying the relevant legal instruments.
in writing. − i would like to draw your attention to a seldom-emphasised aspect of evaluating the progress of the countries in question: the situation of roma. realising the importance of establishing the legal framework for anti-discrimination, by 2004 the eu managed to enforce some real improvements through hardening the accession conditions for new member states. i am happy to see that the progress reports are rather critical about the achievements of these three countries, revealing that only croatia was able to attain some slight improvement in fostering the social inclusion of roma. candidate countries must be involved in the european-level pursuit of roma integration before now, since accession negotiations grant an unparalleled opportunity to trigger a substantial shift in the governmental attitudes towards granting equal access to roma in employment, education, housing and health care, promoting political participation and empowering the romani civil movement. all european countries - present and future members of the european union - must engage in a concerted effort in order to overcome the historical social exclusion of the continent’s largest ethnic minority and sign up for a community action plan, which provides the proper legal leverage to force the participants to comply with their own pledges.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. my fellow members of the house of representatives! the range of security measures at international airports is constantly expanding as aviation security, our security, faces new and new challenges. however, many elements of the measures already taken or planned and tested to improve aviation safety go beyond the strictly necessary aviation safety measures. these issues should therefore be examined in their full context. safety is the key to our lives, the most important thing. but we feel safe only if, in the process, our rights are not disproportionately restricted, our privacy is not violated, our health is not harmed where appropriate, and, overall, the measures taken to ensure our safety are not disproportionate, unavoidable and sufficiently effective. we need tools, the use of which can be useful in body scanners: - ensures effective control of passenger traffic; - is essentially voluntary, taking into account, among others, travellers with vital medical implants (e.g. electric heart rate regulators or metal implants); - is not harmful to health, neither to children, nor to pregnant women, nor to persons who travel frequently; - does not record visual data, but is only used to provide an appropriate alert in the event of a risk. therefore, any further debate on the introduction of test scanners is only meaningful if the impact studies have been prepared which adequately analyse the above conditions. only the use of appropriate means can be acceptable to us.

in writing. a new chapter in eu-ukraine relations after the presidential elections one of the main areas of cooperation will continue to be energy supply. this is no coincidence, as the increasingly dependent union is channelling a significant proportion of its energy imports through its eastern neighbour. in order to ensure energy imports, i believe it is appropriate for the european union to assist ukraine by all possible means in maintaining and renewing its energy network, in particular the vital gas pipeline system. however, in line with the conclusions of the eu summit in june, i believe that the financial support has one basic condition: ukraine must start reforming the industry, make the gas industry more transparent. the commission has also found that the ukrainian authorities have not provided any evidence that the union industry has been affected by the anti-dumping measures in force. however, efforts to increase storage capacity should also be supported. in fact, backup training is one of the main guarantees of uninterrupted european gas transit during the cold winter period. the european parliament is expected to give its blessing to the new eu gas regulation in the spring. the legislation will require member states to adopt, in consultation with each other, action plans in the event of gas supply disruptions. i believe that not only do member states need to coordinate their action plans with each other, but that ukraine also needs to be involved in the consultation process. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the eu's trade policy is not distorted by the threat of a trade war.
in writing. the european union must play a more active role in ukraine, breaking with the practice of recent years, when the european union has dealt with one of europe's largest states with uncertainty and half-heartedness. ukraine is a key partner of the european union's eastern neighbourhood policy and the most important transit country in our relations with russia. with the election of viktor yanukovych as president, ukraine has a strong, calm leader who is open to both europe and russia. the new president's main task will be to bridge the political divisions that divide the country, to build a stable government and to initiate long overdue comprehensive social and economic reforms. the european union's priority now is to make its presence in ukraine more felt than ever and to put cooperation on a new footing. the eastern partnership provides an excellent framework for this and the external action service established under the lisbon treaty provides the appropriate tools. i would like to emphasise that hungary, whose largest neighbour is ukraine, has a strong interest in creating and maintaining political and economic stability in ukraine. we also have an interest in the consolidation of relations between ukraine and russia. we also strongly hope that ukraine will abandon its anti-minority policy which has undermined the rights of transcarpathian minorities, including the hungarians.
i want to talk about two issues: the trade deficit and co2 emissions. the article rightly points out that the growth of the trade deficits of the european union and the united states contributed greatly to the global economic crisis. the european union increased its trade deficit from 75 billion to 243 billion between 2004 and 2008. triple in four years. this means that each european citizen consumed €500 more than he or she produced in 2008. such a trade deficit, consumption exceeding production to such an extent, cannot be sustained. so we need to take action to reduce the trade deficit, because if we don't do it consciously, the laws of economics will spontaneously force it out. and the social cost of that is much, much higher. what is happening in greece right now is an example of this. as regards energy, it is not only protectionism but also price support for energy sources that distorts world trade. many developing countries promote the use of oil, obtain it at a lower price than the world market price, and also support the use of electricity from budgetary resources. meanwhile, in europe, producers have to pay taxes, they have to pay carbon quotas if they want to use energy for production. it became clear in copenhagen that the developing countries are blocking the climate agreement, they are not willing to accept the sanctions that come with the reduction. measures to circumvent this need to be taken, because now several economists point out that china has a greater comparative advantage in cheap carbon emissions than in cheap labour. if we don't reduce our energy consumption, we risk being repeatedly... (the president shall withdraw the floor. )
the fundamental question is whether global free trade, the current system led by the wto, is good for people, or whether we should think about a new governing principle that would not necessarily be called protectionism, but rather economic self-determination. i would suggest that we look at exactly what these two different governing principles mean in three areas. what does global trade in its current form and scale mean for job creation and retention in europe, especially when we think of the retailers, small businesses, family businesses, small farmers in european countries who are losing and losing their opportunities because of the current global, liberalised world trade? so when we think about solidarity, is it good for poor, developing countries to have to open up their markets instead of developing their agriculture, their industry? is it good for the environment to have to transport it over such distances? i would suggest that as a first step, at least in terms of agriculture and food, we think hard about introducing the principle of food self-determination, which means that communities, countries, have the right to decide what they want to produce, how they want to produce it, how they want to sell it. and consumers have a right to good quality, healthy, nutritious food, which we know is not food shipped thousands of miles away, but food that is locally grown, locally processed and locally sold.
in writing. the contraction in world trade has particularly affected the countries of central and eastern europe, as the decline in demand has been concentrated on products (automobiles, consumer electronics, etc.) which have played a significant role in exports. at the same time, these countries were also strongly affected by the credit crisis. in order to overcome the difficulties of the double constraint and the resulting social problems (e.g. rising unemployment), it would be very important to boost exports. in this respect, the financing of exporters in particular appears to be a significant obstacle, given the delayed payment deadlines, the increased cost and difficulty of obtaining credit and the deteriorated conditions of credit insurance. therefore, programmes should be developed for these countries, in particular for eu member states, which could help overcome these difficulties.
i would like to extend a warm welcome to commissioner barnier, who i would like to wish a good job. ladies and gentlemen, fellow members of parliament, allow me to sum up my remarks in three points. first of all, in today's world of mass media and the internet, in this world of expert analysis, gambling, and especially online gambling, cannot be talked about without its social, cultural and mental health effects. it is also clear from the parliament's decision adopted a year ago that online gambling has clear harmful social consequences. it is enough to talk about the effects of addiction, of organized crime, of money laundering, and not to forget the poisoning effect of sports betting, and through sports, because we are still living in a terrible european football scandal, which unfortunately is related to this issue. secondly, i think we're on the wrong side of the horse when we think of the regulation of online gambling as a free-market issue. this is primarily a consumer protection issue. in my view, the regulation should focus on consumer protection issues. and last but not least, let me make two suggestions. common regulation at european level is needed, one that starts from the point of view of consumer protection and puts prevention at its core: preventing the development of addictive diseases, preventing the link between online gambling and organised crime and also preventing the 'fair play' scandals. last but not least, there is a need for the european union to initiate a cross-border, cross-sector regulation, because the issue of online gambling is a global issue, so i think we need to address that as well.

my fellow members of the house of representatives! as a member of the presidency trio, as the hungarian representative, i am following with great appreciation the activities of president van rompuy, and the strong european commitment and faith with which he has taken up his new role. it is now up to us to decide how the presidency of the european council will function. i believe that europe, in its crisis, needs a strong hand and a strong direction. the credibility of the new programme is also a matter of concern, as it will not suffer the fate of its predecessor, the lisbon strategy. i say this as a representative of a former communist country. there is a natural resistance to loud, long-term plans in that part of europe - perhaps understandably. i have an institutional and a substantive comment. the institutional one: we need to decide who is the target of this strategy. if you're the leaders of the union, then what's happened so far is appropriate, and the tight schedule is good. but if we think that the target group is the citizens of the union, whom we want to win over, with whom we want to build a union that is stronger, more competitive and able to offer more to its citizens than it is today, then we must act in accordance with the treaty of lisbon and fairly involve the european parliament and, i would go further, the national parliaments. today's debate is no substitute for dealing with the subject, the rapporteur's assertion, in the usual way in the committees and in the political groups in this house. and as far as content is concerned, the main objective should be job creation. it's the only place to start any new strategy. how we do that, we don't know much about yet. we know that there are fewer priorities, identifying bottlenecks, closer economic policy coordination. that would be fine, but i want you to consider the following: number one, don't throw away what's worked so far. the eu has been made stronger by the policies it has adopted and it is contrary to the founding treaties to abandon the cohesion and agricultural policies which serve the interests of the citizens of the union. secondly, the new strategy is in the interest of all regions, not just individual companies or countries. cohesion also increases the union's competitiveness. third, we tailor the strategy to the country, and that's how we make it credible.
in writing. the european union's 2020 strategy can only succeed if the member states show sufficient commitment to its implementation. in addition to strong national commitments, the key to the success of the new strategy is the community policies and the use of the resources allocated to them for containment, rural development and agriculture, which contribute to economic growth, job creation. the emerging top-down approach, based on the prime minister's political accountability, also offers a greater guarantee of success than the lisbon strategy. the 2020 strategy also sets out the priorities for the next budgetary period, without the possibility of a detailed debate now. accordingly, the importance of the common agricultural policy and cohesion policy should be emphasised now in the seven-year budget starting in 2014. without economic, social and territorial cohesion, there is no strong, competitive european union. convergence between regions strengthens the eu's competitiveness.
in writing. - ladies and gentlemen of the house. the european union is facing a major challenge in the revision of the lisbon strategy and, in this context, in the fight against poverty and exclusion and the strengthening of social cohesion. the eu 2020 initiative, which is one of the key pillars of the spanish-belgian-hungarian presidency trio's programme, must respond to the continent's long-term demographic and social challenges, which can mean nothing less than a rethinking of the european labour market and education system. in the face of the failure of the european employment strategy so far, the eu 2020 programme should indeed create more and better jobs, with the priority objective of increasing the participation of women and vulnerable groups in the labour market. it is encouraging that both the european commission's working document and the programme of the successive presidencies focus on factors that are essential to the success of the programme, such as tackling undeclared work, the black economy and early school leaving, and improving self-employment conditions. as socioeconomic exclusion is the result of the interaction of many factors, the only solution is to create a comprehensive action plan that addresses all areas together, rather than the project-based approaches that have been used so far. instead of isolated initiatives, these measures need to be combined to succeed in a balanced policy package that focuses on early intervention and can deliver substantial improvements in each of the local indicators reflecting the real extent of social exclusion.
in writing. the eu 2020 strategy should be based on two pillars. on the one hand, solidarity between member states, which guarantees that no member state is left behind in the crisis. on the other hand, individual member states must also show solidarity with the community by implementing the obligations defined jointly. in the debate on the future of the european union, we must always keep in mind the community policies that are already in place. the common agricultural policy and the cohesion policy are the real fruits of european integration, symbols of solidarity between the member states and nations. as a representative of hungary and as a politician from a new member state, i consider the new priorities set out by the european commission to be important: building a knowledge-based society, promoting innovation, strengthening social inclusion, creating new jobs and taking stronger action against climate change in the context of sustainable development. however, the strengthening of cohesion in these areas must not come at the expense of the weakening of previous community policies. food and food security guaranteed by the cap will become increasingly important in the 21st century, when food and freshwater have become as strategic as oil in the 20th century. if we really want to strengthen the eu's global competitiveness, we cannot allow some regions to fall apart because of their lagging infrastructure, poor education, social and health services. a strong cohesion policy based on solidarity is therefore still needed.

on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, in 2010 we mark the 15th anniversary of the launch of the beijing platform for action. this is a moment of reflection where we need not only to evaluate what we have achieved so far but also to think about new ways of attaining the goals set out in 1995. the beginning was reassuring: in 1995 a global consensus was reached according to which women and men were equal from all points of view, including political, economic, legal and social. however, despite this consensus, the sequel is less stirring. many of the goals of the beijing platform for action are still far from being reached. in many countries women are still not sufficiently empowered and poverty has a feminine face. moreover, even in the 21st century, even in developed countries, we notice again debate on some basic issues, questioning already-established rights such as sexual and reproductive health rights. i am very happy that the european parliament is not following such trends but, on the contrary, committing itself to these basic rights. nevertheless, the political debates, the conservative attacks on human rights are alarming and they clearly underline the fact that the fight for women’s rights is far from over. i do not think that human rights can be subject to compromise. we must not put up with the lowest common denominator. therefore, when we go to new york in a few days’ time, we have to make efforts so that all women have the same rights and that these rights are protected. i will personally do this under the motto ‘my body, my right’, which we have to convey to all the women of the world.
in writing. every day, a member state or a large company announces major investment plans in the energy sector. there are dozens of different gas pipelines, offshore wind farms and power plants on the drawing board. however, coordination between future investments leaves much to be desired. this is the only reason why the regulation under discussion should be supported, which would provide a uniform structure for the reporting obligations of the member states in relation to energy investments. the legislation to be adopted will allow for the coordination of regional investments and facilitate joint planning. it can thus strengthen the eu's single energy market and security of supply. i consider it important that the reporting obligations in the present proposal do not impose an excessive administrative burden on the authorities of the member states. it should be ensured that the method of reporting complies with previously adopted standards. in this respect, it is worth recalling that the eu directives on the internal market for electricity and natural gas already require the preparation of 10-year investment plans. the original proposal of the european commission did not address district heating investments. the amendment proposed by parliament's committee on industry to include this as one of the areas subject to reporting obligations should therefore be supported. we must not forget that district heating plays a particularly important role in the provision of health care in the new member states. in hungary, for example, it provides food for some two million people. investments in district heating cannot therefore be disregarded when coordinating energy policy investments.
in writing. mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, although the implementation of investment projects in energy infrastructure is already subject to serious uncertainties and the current economic and credit crisis is creating serious difficulties for investment projects in the energy sector, it should be clear that major investments in energy infrastructure in the european union in the coming years are crucial for the new european energy policy aimed at securing energy supply, mitigating the effects of climate change and maintaining competitiveness. it is an important tool for the development of the common energy policy. we cannot effectively support european energy policy at eu level without having sufficient information about our energy infrastructure. i therefore see it as our common objective to ensure that the community is accurately and regularly informed about investment projects in the eu's energy infrastructure; to alleviate the burden of notification; to increase the usefulness of the analysis carried out on the basis of the information received by the commission; and to alleviate the burden on private sector economic operators, who are playing an increasingly important role in infrastructure development investments.

in writing. − the objectives of the beijing platform for action adopted 15 years ago have not yet been met and little progress has been made in most areas of gender equality. it is regrettable that there is little emphasis on combating the misery and multiple discrimination faced by women, both at member state and eu level. there is a need for much better coordination in the implementation of the beijing targets agreed at the un and the new eu roadmap for equality between men and women. in the european year for combating poverty and social exclusion, it is particularly important to ensure that women living just above the poverty line are adequately protected, as they are at increased risk of becoming impoverished as a result of changes in their employment or family status, such as dismissal, divorce, widowhood or even childbirth. it is gratifying to note that the programme of the next spanish-belgian-hungarian presidency trio places great emphasis on monitoring the implementation of the beijing goals and on a comprehensive approach to preventing and combating poverty among women and children. these aspects will hopefully be given due weight at the un meeting in early march. at the same time, gender-disaggregated and reliable data are needed to evaluate and review policies to achieve equality of opportunity between men and women, and the introduction of standardised common indicators to measure gender inequality should be considered.
i would like to draw the attention of my fellow members to the fact that it is a huge mistake to speak in general terms about european women and the rights of women in the european union and not to notice the second-class status of women in the post-communist countries of central and eastern europe, which are newly joining the union. it is time for the european union to carry out fact-finding research into their special deprivation of rights and to use this research to remedy the serious discrimination between women and women in europe. let me mention just two areas. both of these areas are linked to globalization, neoliberal economics, liberalized free trade and its consequences. first, multinational companies and large european companies registered in the european union apply very different standards of labour law and health and safety at work in their companies. i'm thinking of companies like big retailers, hypermarkets, even on both sides of the austro-hungarian border. the same company has very different standards for rest periods, lunch periods, termination. he practically enslaves his female workers from eastern europe, some of them hungarian. the other area that is completely unexplored is the migration of women within the european union. second-class central and eastern european women - such as hungarian women - live in the ranks of domestic workers, as economic refugees in western european households, where they are often forced to go as domestic workers, leaving their children behind. their situation, in terms of their rights, in terms of their dignity, is much more similar to the situation of women in developing countries in the third world than to the situation of their sisters in western europe who are citizens of a member state of the european union. this needs urgent attention.

on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commissioner is here. i welcome the fact that we are taking a position in this parliament on a matter which could facilitate the travel of legally resident third-country nationals within the union, and i congratulate my colleague mr coelho on the excellent work he has done here in parliament on this matter. this proposal aims to facilitate and make tangible the travel within the union of third-country nationals holding a so-called d visa, which entitles them to stay for a long period in one of the member states. this will provide a solution to situations where, for various reasons, some member states are unable or unwilling to issue residence permits in good time to third-country nationals residing on their territory. so they are not using the schengen framework properly. and i'm glad we can move forward on this issue. our aim is to ensure that third-country nationals who enter the european union do not experience the fortress of impenetrability. integrated border management and visa policy should serve these objectives. as the hungarian representative, i urge that the external borders of the union be open to travellers of good faith. do not restrict the communication between people on both sides of the border. it is in the fundamental interest of third-country nationals living in the neighbourhood of the union, including members of the hungarian minority, to be able to reside legally in the territory of the member states of the union without any bureaucratic or administrative burden. however, this requires appropriate legislation at both community and member state level, but one that does not limit the scope of the other but reinforces the objectives of the other. i hope that the new community legislation will not only be clear in principle but will also make it easier in practice, particularly for young people, university students, who wish to study in the member states. they should be the primary beneficiaries of the regulation. and in this context, i can only agree that the commission should report on the implementation of the regulation by april 2012 at the latest and, if necessary, propose amendments to the regulation in order to meet the objectives set.
mr. president, i have to go. i would like to say that i agree with and support the rapporteur and mrs malmström in finding a solution to this issue that respects the rule of law, respect for human rights, data protection and, of course, security. so much has been said. i'd like to emphasize a few things. for this regulation to work, we first need to study and know more about the practice in each member state. we know that some member states can issue residence permits for a limited period. these good practices can mean a lot to us. we need to coordinate the practices of the member states, which obviously has a confidence-building effect. it is absolutely essential that all countries use the sis system we know that not all countries use it today and above all it is urgent that we can introduce sis ii as soon as possible and that the vis system works, because this will be the guarantee in the long run. it is in the european interest that students, businessmen and researchers from third countries who are here can move freely.

on behalf of the ppe group. as well as more effective application of international and national anti-discrimination legislation, as the commissioner has said, the economic aspects of roma social inclusion will have to be given a strong focus in cordoba, as roma integration into the labour market and education is also a primary economic interest of the member states. in recent years, several international organisations have developed forward-looking plans, but these have not been implemented in the member states due to the lack of adequate binding force, the prospect of possible sanctions and the lack of appropriate budget allocation. the european union is able to ensure that a community strategy beyond the so-called soft low instruments is developed and properly implemented and evaluated on the basis of clear indicators. as rapporteur for the european roma strategy in the european parliament, i consider it extremely important that the strategy identifies crisis areas in the member states where immediate action is needed. social disadvantages are unevenly distributed across geographical areas and micro-regions with a high density of roma and non-roma people are concentrated in poverty and social exclusion, which is a serious obstacle to social development in europe. such areas should be lifted out of the competitive fray and developed through intensive programmes tailored to their specific needs. in line with the principle of subsidiarity, the monitoring and control of the strategy should be carried out by local organisations and i would suggest that extensive surveys of the needs of local target groups should be carried out, similar to the irish agricultural tenders. the collection of ethnically disaggregated and independently evaluated statistical data is also essential for evaluating the outcome of the programme. the european people's party believes that these are the key points towards which we feel the cordoba high-level meeting should point.
because i speak in my native language, i use the word "gypsy" instead of "roma", which is not a pejorative term, and is also written in our constitution. the subject of the agenda is the exclusion and discrimination of the roma. the social integration of the roma is a fundamental condition for the solution. school is an important means of doing so. in many cases, separate employment is justified, if you like positive discrimination, to overcome disadvantages. minority rights advocates are quick to call for segregation, when the goal is to integrate as quickly as possible. in some regions of hungary, generations of roma are growing up with families who live on welfare rather than on work. there is no way out without creating jobs, so we must break with neoliberal economic policies. nor can a difficult social situation justify breaking the law. in hungary, the proportion of roma in crime is high. this calls for a strong response, not only from the majority of society, but also from the decent majority of the roma. as occam, we have never identified a bloodline, a genetic cause. we're not talking about racism, we're just talking about specific socio-cultural circumstances, like background. so if we reflexively label those who say that as racist, we're just sweeping the problem under the rug. we have to find a way out together. to do this, the roma need leaders - because they can't do without them - who are credible in their own circles and in the eyes of society as a whole. we do need a common european strategy, but one that faces all aspects of this issue and is sincere about its solution.
i think the second summit in cordoba is a really good opportunity to take account of what has happened in recent years in terms of the inclusion of europe's largest and most vulnerable community, the roma community. first of all, i would like to stress that we have taken very important steps towards making this a truly european issue. it is not a central-eastern european issue, but a pan-european one. to find solutions, we have to treat it this way. the adoption by the european parliament of a resolution on the need for a roma strategy was an important step in this direction. it is regrettable that this has not yet happened, and we sincerely hope that serious progress will be made in this area during the presidency of the trio and during the term of this commission. the 2009 european parliament report on the social and labour market situation of roma was also important, as was the programme for the roma decade, which we are now about halfway through. for the strategy to be successful, it is necessary to see how the structural and cohesion funds have been used, whether they have really contributed and under what conditions to the social inclusion of the roma community. we need data, but we know that ethnicity data is always sensitive data, and we have to be careful when we ask for it and how we handle it. while there are many positive steps, we see that there are many problems with the implementation of readmission agreements. we've already had kosovo. i sincerely hope that the summit will help to resolve these issues.
as the drafter of europe's first roma medium-term government programme and rapporteur for the 2002 council of europe roma report, i agree that this is one of the most complex issues in europe today. i do not envy the commissioner, because there is no more complex, more complicated issue in europe today. there is evidence that the roma are not simply an ethnic and national minority, but a socially disadvantaged minority in many ways. however, it is not clear what the distribution of responsibility is between the majority and the minority. responsibility is asymmetric between the majority and the minority, because the majority has a much greater responsibility, but the minority has a responsibility, the roma have a responsibility. fourth, very important, we need integration without assimilation. and finally, it is not a question of budget, it is not a question of resources, but it is a question of whether there are people, mechanisms, both from the point of view of the majority and the roma society. as a former secretary of state, i have to say, the minority issue worked where there was a committed local majority figure, a credible local minority leader who could mobilize the minority, and the ngos helped. it's not about the money. we need the eu level, but things are being done at the national and local levels, and that is why i support the eu strategy.

in writing. i welcome the fact that the european commission has launched a process with the announcement of the first railway package, which can be seen as a first step towards the standardisation of the railway service at european level. however, there is concern that the transposition of the three directives that make up this package by the member states has caused serious difficulties for 21 countries, which may hinder the proper transposition of the subsequent packages. i would like to draw the european commission's attention to a contradiction between the high european-level economic and efficiency requirements for rail networks and the positive impact of rail on regional development, the mobility of rural residents and people with disabilities and the environment. i suggest that the commission should find a balance and compromise between these two contradictions, bearing in mind the need to clarify the principle of cost sharing between the member states and the european union and the importance of creating a single transport system in the community. it is necessary to establish a healthy competition involving all the players in the sector, where the real competition is not between individual transport sectors but between individual and collective transport.

my fellow members of parliament! the european central bank faced unprecedented challenges in its operations in 2008 when the global economic crisis hit europe in september 2008. the financial crisis turned into a real economic crisis, as the interbank money market froze due to mistrust, and financial institutions did not lend to each other or to companies. the ecb has responded to financial developments swiftly and, in my view, appropriately, but we cannot ignore an unfortunate fact in the assessment of crisis management: discrimination between non-euro area countries. i am convinced that the ecb acted contrary to the spirit of the union when, at the height of the crisis in october 2008, it did not ensure a level playing field for the use of liquidity facilities. with the swedish and danish national banks, this ecb carried out a money-exchange operation in order to provide the banking systems of the two countries with adequate euro liquidity, whereas with the hungarian and polish central banks it was only willing to carry out the same operation with the requirement of collateral. this move by the ecb has unfortunately contributed to market uncertainty and further aggravated the situation of the countries concerned. now that we're working to create a new financial system, we need to take care in advance to eliminate these inequalities. we cannot therefore make rules which affect some parts of the union more adversely than others. i would like to take this idea further when i talk about the european systemic risk board, which we are all working on creating. the ecb will play a significant role in this. the presidency of the council will also be held by the president of the ecb, and we will need to ensure that euro-zone and non-euro-zone countries, and thus the countries of central and eastern europe, will have the same quality of voting rights in the new body. i fear that if we do not speak out against the discrimination we have already experienced
